================================================
FILE: README.md
================================================
# SearchJev

Training code for **SearchJev: A Fast and Calibrated System-1 Model for Search Agents**.

SearchJev turns the short, structured decisions of a search agent (is this document relevant? is the
evidence sufficient? where should the next query go? which link next?) into schema-conditioned typed
prediction. Given a search state and a decision schema, it returns a calibrated probability
distribution over the legal values of every field in a single forward pass of a Qwen3.5 backbone,
without autoregressive decoding.

Everything is open: code, data, model weights, and the complete training logs.

| | |
|---|---|
| Paper | [SearchJev (PDF)](SearchJev.pdf) |
| Data (SearchDecision-Bench) and model weights | <https://huggingface.co/SearchJev> |
| Training logs (W&B report, full training runs) | [Search_Jev report](https://forge.coreweave.com/wandb/R-DTeam/Search_JEV/reports/Search_Jev-Report--VmlldzoxODAzMTUwMw) |

This repository contains the complete training pipeline used for the paper models
(SearchJev-0.8B and SearchJev-4B; the same recipe also trains the 9B backbone):

1. **data conversion**: the SearchDecision-Bench release -> per-dataset JEV files (`tools/convert_unified_v2.py`);
2. **data preparation**: schema instantiation, option permutation, label-reliability weights,
   deduplication and removal of any train/val state that also occurs in a test split (`training/data.py`);
3. **training**: LoRA on Qwen3.5, restricted first-token option-label readout, soft-label
   cross-entropy + 0.5 x Brier score (`training/loss.py`, `training/trainer.py`), single GPU or DDP;
4. **calibration**: one temperature per question type fitted on half of the validation data,
   and a held-out evaluation on the other half (`training/calibration.py`).

## Repository layout

```
start_training.py          one-click entry point: prepare -> train -> calibrate -> held-out eval -> summary
config.yaml                the paper recipe (all hyper-parameters; overridable with --set key=value)
training/
  config.py                config loading, presets, validation
  schema.py, labels.py     JEV rows -> typed decision examples; single-token option labels
  prompting.py             prompt rendering (state + question + options)
  data.py, collator.py     data preparation, caching, length-grouped batching
  modeling.py              Qwen3.5 + LoRA, restricted option-label readout
  loss.py, trainer.py      soft-label CE + Brier, transformers.Trainer subclass
  calibration.py           temperature scaling
  inference.py             JEVPredictor (batched inference with a trained run)
  metrics.py, gpus.py, bootstrap.py
tools/convert_unified_v2.py
```

## Installation

Python 3.12 on Linux with CUDA GPUs (the paper runs used 6x NVIDIA H200). The Qwen3.5 linear-attention
kernels (`flash-linear-attention`) require a GPU.

```bash
python3.12 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -r requirements.txt          # or: uv sync  (exact versions from pyproject.toml)
```

`start_training.py` re-executes itself with `./.venv/bin/python` (or `$JEV_PYTHON`) when it is started
with an interpreter that lacks torch.

## Data preparation

SearchDecision-Bench is trained on; the SearchJev-V1 families (`classification`, `ranking`, `search`)
are used only for validation and calibration.

```bash
# 1. SearchDecision-Bench (train / val / test)
hf download SearchJev/search_jev_unified_v2_release_20260928 --repo-type dataset \
    --local-dir data/search_jev_unified_v2_release_20260928
python tools/convert_unified_v2.py          # -> data/SearchJev-V2/unified_v2/<dataset>.jsonl

# 2. SearchJev-V1 (validation-only families)
hf download SearchJev/SearchJev-V1 --repo-type dataset --local-dir data/SearchJev-V1
for f in classification ranking search; do ln -s ../SearchJev-V1/$f data/SearchJev-V2/$f; done
```

Without SearchJev-V1, add `--set data.val_only_families=[]` (validation and calibration then use the
SearchDecision-Bench validation split only).

Data preparation runs automatically at the start of training and is cached under `data/processed/`;
`--prepare-only` stops after it. Rows whose split is `test` are never used for training or validation.

## Training

```bash
# one model (all visible GPUs; DDP via torchrun when more than one GPU is selected)
python start_training.py --model 4B --gpus 0,1,2,3,4,5

# the paper models, one after another (output: outputs/searchjev-{0.8b,4b})
python start_training.py --models 0.8B,4B --gpus 0,1,2,3,4,5

# log to Weights & Biases
python start_training.py --model 4B --wandb SearchJev

# inspect the resolved configuration and data sources without doing anything
python start_training.py --model 4B --dry-run

# end-to-end smoke test on one GPU (0.8B, a few hundred examples, 20 steps)
python start_training.py --debug

# resume from the latest checkpoint
python start_training.py --model 4B --resume auto
```

Any config key can be overridden, e.g. `--set training.learning_rate=1e-4`.

### Paper recipe

| | SearchJev-0.8B / 4B |
|---|---|
| Backbone | Qwen3.5-0.8B / 4B (instruct) |
| Tuning | LoRA, rank 16, alpha 32, dropout 0.05 (attention, Gated DeltaNet and MLP projections) |
| Optimizer | AdamW (fused), lr 5e-5, weight decay 0.01, cosine schedule, 5% warm-up, grad clip 1.0, bf16 |
| Batch | 22 per GPU x 6 GPUs = 132; 1 epoch = 5,176 steps over 683,130 training decisions |
| Max input length | 2,048 tokens (only the state is truncated) |
| Loss | soft-label cross-entropy + 0.5 x Brier score |
| Label weight | 0.5 for LLM-assigned labels, demonstrations and constructed rewrites |
| Calibration | one temperature per question type on 5,000 validation decisions |

With a different number of GPUs, keep the effective batch at 132
(`per_device_train_batch_size x gradient_accumulation_steps x #GPUs`).

## Outputs

```
outputs/searchjev-4b/
  adapter/                 LoRA adapter (+ tokenizer)
  calibration.json         per-question-type temperatures
  label_space.json         option-label alphabet
  config.resolved.yaml     the exact configuration of the run
  prepare_stats.json       data-preparation statistics
  train_metrics.json, train.log
  eval_val/metrics.json    held-out validation metrics before / after temperature scaling
  summary.json
```

## Inference

```python
from training.inference import JEVPredictor

predictor = JEVPredictor("outputs/searchjev-4b", device="cuda", merge_lora=True)
result = predictor.decide(
    state={"question": "When was the Eiffel Tower completed?",
           "documents": ["The Eiffel Tower was completed in March 1889 for the World's Fair."]},
    question="Is the evidence sufficient to answer the question now?",
    question_type="noul",                      # noul (yes/no) | choice | score
)
print(result.choice, result.confidence, result.probabilities)
```

`predictor.answer_jev(row)` answers every question of a row in the JEV format used by the data files.

## Citation

@misc{cao2026searchjev,
  title  = {{SearchJev}: A Fast and Calibrated System-1 Model for Search Agents},

FILE: config.yaml
================================================
# SearchJev training configuration.
#
# These values reproduce the paper models (SearchJev-0.8B / 4B / 9B): LoRA on Qwen3.5 instruct
# backbones, one epoch over the SearchDecision-Bench training split (unified_v2), effective batch
# 132 (22 per device x 6 GPUs), soft-label cross-entropy + 0.5 x Brier, per-question-type
# temperature scaling on validation data.
#
# Resolution order (later wins): embedded defaults (training/config.py) -> this file
# -> presets[model.size] -> --set key=value overrides -> CLI flags (--model, --gpus, ...).

run:
  name: searchjev-{size}           # output dir = <output_root>/<name>; {size} -> 0.8b / 4b / 9b
  output_root: outputs             # relative paths are resolved against the repository root
  seed: 42                         # data sub-sampling, option permutation and training
  hf_home: null                    # HF cache directory to export as HF_HOME (null = keep the environment's)
  report_to: none                  # none | wandb | tensorboard  (--wandb PROJECT turns wandb on)
  wandb_project: SearchJev
  wandb_entity: null               # null = default entity of `wandb login` (or $WANDB_ENTITY)

model:
  size: 4B                         # 0.8B | 2B | 4B | 9B | 27B -> Qwen/Qwen3.5-<size>[-Base]
  variant: instruct                # instruct | base
  name: null                       # explicit HF id or local path; overrides size/variant
  dtype: bfloat16                  # bfloat16 | float16 | float32
  attn_implementation: sdpa        # implementation of the full-attention layers
  load_in_4bit: false              # QLoRA (bitsandbytes nf4)
  gradient_checkpointing: true

lora:
  enabled: true                    # false -> full fine-tuning
  r: 16
  alpha: 32
  dropout: 0.05
  # attention, Gated DeltaNet (in_proj_* / out_proj) and MLP projections
  target_modules: [q_proj, k_proj, v_proj, o_proj, gate_proj, up_proj, down_proj, in_proj_qkv, in_proj_z, in_proj_b, in_proj_a, out_proj]
  init_adapter: null               # continue from an existing adapter directory

labels:
  alphabet: extended               # basic (A-Z) | extended (~170 single-token labels) | explicit list
  max_options: null                # max options per question; null = alphabet size
  over_limit_policy: drop          # drop | subsample (keep the gold option + a random subset)

prompt:
  style: auto                      # auto (chat template if available) | chat | plain
  language: bilingual              # en | zh | bilingual (system-prompt wording)
  max_length: 2048                 # token budget; only the STATE is truncated, options never are

data:
  root: data/SearchJev-V2          # <root>/<family>/<dataset>.jsonl (see README: data preparation)
  mode: sampled                    # sampled | full (global; family_modes overrides per family)
  families: [unified_v2]           # trained on (train + val): SearchDecision-Bench
  family_modes:
    unified_v2: full               # unified_v2 has no sampled subset
  val_only_families: [classification, ranking, search]   # SearchJev-V1: validation / calibration only
  processed_dir: data/processed    # cache: <processed_dir>/<mode>/<family>/{train,val}.jsonl
  train_splits: [train]            # never 'test'
  val_split: val
  val_fallback_per_dataset: 300    # sampled mode: val rows streamed per dataset when a subset has none
  max_val_per_dataset: 500
  max_train_per_dataset: null
  dedup_val_against_train: true    # drop val examples identical to a training example
  exclude_test_overlap: true       # drop train/val examples whose state occurs in a test row of any prepared family
  include_datasets: null
  exclude_datasets: []
  permutation:                     # shuffle options of unordered fields during training (position bias)
    choice: true
    noul: true
    score: false                   # ordinal scales keep their order
    copies: 1
  prepare_workers: 8
  rebuild: false

training:
  epochs: 1
  per_device_train_batch_size: 22  # x 6 GPUs = 132 (presets below set the same value per size)
  per_device_eval_batch_size: 32
  gradient_accumulation_steps: 1   # effective batch = per_device x accum x number of GPUs
  learning_rate: 5.0e-5
  weight_decay: 0.01
  warmup_ratio: 0.05
  lr_scheduler_type: cosine
  max_grad_norm: 1.0
  bf16: true
  logging_steps: 10
  eval_strategy: steps             # no | steps | epoch
  eval_steps: 500
  save_steps: 500
  save_total_limit: 3
  max_steps: -1                    # > 0 overrides epochs
  max_train_examples: null
  max_eval_examples: 2000          # in-training evaluation subset of val
  dataloader_num_workers: 8
  group_by_length: true            # batch prompts of similar length together (less padding)
  optim: adamw_torch_fused
  resume_from_checkpoint: null     # null | auto | path

loss:
  type: ce                         # ce | kl (soft-label cross-entropy or KL to the soft target)
  lambda_brier: 0.5                # L = CE + lambda_brier * Brier
  sample_weights:                  # training-loss weights by label reliability (null = unweighted)
    weak: 0.5                      # rows with meta.weak (demonstrations, constructed rewrites, LLM labels)
    label_tier:
      llm_teacher: 0.5             # LLM-teacher labels; the minimum of all matching weights is used

calibration:
  enabled: true                    # temperature scaling after training
  fraction: 0.5                    # val is split by id hash: this part fits the temperatures
  per_question_type: true          # one temperature per question type (+ a global fallback)
  max_examples: 5000

evaluation:
  max_examples: 5000               # held-out half of val evaluated after training (raw vs calibrated)

distributed:
  backend: auto                    # auto | none | ddp (auto: torchrun DDP when > 1 GPU is selected)
  num_gpus: all                    # all | N (N < visible: the GPUs with the most free memory)
  gpus: null                       # explicit ids, e.g. "0,1,2,3,4,5" (wins over num_gpus)
  gpu_selection: free              # free | first
  min_free_gb: 16                  # only GPUs with at least this much free memory are used

presets:
  "0.8B": {training: {per_device_train_batch_size: 22, gradient_accumulation_steps: 1}}
  "2B":   {training: {per_device_train_batch_size: 22, gradient_accumulation_steps: 1}}
  "4B":   {training: {per_device_train_batch_size: 22, gradient_accumulation_steps: 1}}
  "9B":   {training: {per_device_train_batch_size: 22, gradient_accumulation_steps: 1}}
  "27B":  {training: {per_device_train_batch_size: 1, gradient_accumulation_steps: 32, per_device_eval_batch_size: 4}}

FILE: start_training.py
